# computed-ratios: BIOCON, rows closed by computation from held data

Generated by soic-ladder `scripts/case_study_compute_rows.py`; do not edit by hand. Nothing here is fetched. Every result below is **DERIVED**:
computed in code from the held inputs listed under it, never a filed number and never a third-party print (where a third-party figure is an input,
the input table says so). Conventions: FY22 is the year ended 31 March 2022; money is Rs million as filed unless the unit column says otherwise;
consolidated unless the basis column says otherwise. `not held` means no value in the held files for that item; nothing is estimated in its place.
`n/m` means the base is zero or negative, so a growth rate has no meaning. A comparison with a bar is a comparison, not a verdict, and a date match is
not a cause.

## L2-54: Five-year ROCE block against the 15% and 20% bars

**Formula in words.** Capital employed = total assets - total current liabilities at the year end. ROCE (pre-tax) = (profit before tax + finance costs) / capital employed x 100. ROCE (EBIT) = ebit_company_basis / capital employed x 100. Worst year = the minimum over FY22-FY26 (the five-year block); FY21 is shown too. Each year and the minimum is set against the two bars.

**Caveats**

- The 15 bar is a gate in the rulebook file; the 20 bar is an observation there. This row only sets the numbers beside them.

## L2-59: Capital employed and profit, start and end of the five-year block

**Formula in words.** Capital employed = total assets - total current liabilities at FY21 and FY26. Change = FY26 - FY21. Profit = profit attributable to owners. CAGR = (FY26 / FY21)^(1/5) - 1. Incremental ratio = change in profit / change in capital employed.

**Inputs (held)**

| Input | Period | Value | Unit | Basis | Source file |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| bs.total_assets | FY21 | 185,223 | Rs million | consolidated | roast-room/docs/company-record/biocon/ar-tables-FY21-FY26.csv (bs.total_assets, filed; AR pdf p264, p308, p346, p348, p362) |
| bs.total_assets | FY26 | 636,506 | Rs million | consolidated | roast-room/docs/company-record/biocon/ar-tables-FY21-FY26.csv (bs.total_assets, filed; AR pdf p264, p308, p346, p348, p362) |
| bs.total_current_liabilities | FY21 | 42,101 | Rs million | consolidated | roast-room/docs/company-record/biocon/ar-tables-FY21-FY26.csv (bs.total_current_liabilities, filed; AR pdf p264, p308, p346, p348, p362) |
| bs.total_current_liabilities | FY26 | 140,692 | Rs million | consolidated | roast-room/docs/company-record/biocon/ar-tables-FY21-FY26.csv (bs.total_current_liabilities, filed; AR pdf p264, p308, p346, p348, p362) |
| pl.profit_attributable_to_owners | FY21 | 7,405 | Rs million | consolidated | roast-room/docs/company-record/biocon/ar-tables-FY21-FY26.csv (pl.profit_attributable_to_owners, filed; AR pdf p265, p309, p347, p349, p363) |
| pl.profit_attributable_to_owners | FY26 | 3,856 | Rs million | consolidated | roast-room/docs/company-record/biocon/ar-tables-FY21-FY26.csv (pl.profit_attributable_to_owners, filed; AR pdf p265, p309, p347, p349, p363) |

**Computed result, DERIVED**

*Block FY21 to FY26 (Rs million)*

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| capital employed FY21 | 143,122 |
| capital employed FY26 | 495,814 |
| change in capital employed | 352,692 |
| profit to owners FY21 | 7,405 |
| profit to owners FY26 | 3,856 |
| change in profit to owners | -3,549 |
| profit CAGR FY21-FY26, % (5 years) | -12.23 |
| change in profit / change in capital employed, % | -1.01 |

**Caveats**

- A ten-year block (FY16-FY26) is not computed: total assets and current liabilities for FY16-FY20 are not held as extracted values (FY16-FY20: not held).
- FY23 capital employed jumps with the Biologics deal, so the incremental ratio is not a return on organic new money. Profit is the owners' share while capital employed is the whole group.

## L3-47: EBITDA with and without the share-based payment charge

**Formula in words.** EBITDA with the charge = ebitda_company_basis as held (the charge sits inside employee cost, so it is already borne). EBITDA without the charge = ebitda_company_basis + share_based_payment_expense. Charge % = charge / EBITDA with the charge x 100.

## L7-23: Quarterly profit growth and the market-cap screen

**Formula in words.** Growth = profit in the quarter / profit in the same quarter a year earlier - 1, x 100 (n/m where the year-ago profit is zero or negative). Table 1 uses the filed consolidated profit to shareholders; table 2 uses screener's printed quarterly Net Profit (third party). Market cap in crore = market_cap_latest / 10, set against the floor in the rulebook file.

## L7-28: Own median P/B and P/S against the current values

**Formula in words.** Median = middle of the six held year-end values (FY21-FY26) of price_to_book_owners_equity and price_to_sales (recomputed beside them as market_cap / equity and market_cap / revenue from operations). Current P/B = market_cap_latest / FY26 equity attributable to owners. Current P/S = market_cap_latest / FY26 revenue from operations, and / screener's TTM sales.

- The Sep-2026 balance sheet is not held: current P/B uses March 2026 equity against an October 2026 market cap.

## L7-40: EPS growth over ten years and profit growth over ten

**Formula in words.** EPS growth = EPS on the current share basis this year / last year - 1, x 100 (FY17-FY26). FY19-FY26 are the held values. FY16-FY18 are the EPS printed in the annual reports of their own time divided by the bonus ratios (each ratio = EPS before a bonus / the same year's EPS after it, both printed). CAGR = (end / start)^(1/years) - 1. Profit growth and CAGR recomputed from profit attributable to owners, beside the held values.

**Caveats**

- FY16-FY18 are consolidated EPS after exceptional items, bonus-adjusted by dividing by a ratio read from two printed numbers, not a number any report prints on the current basis. FY18 (6.31 printed in the FY18 and FY19 reports) is divided by the 2020 ratio only; the FY18 report's own '*' marks its FY17 comparative as already adjusted for the 2018 bonus.
- FY16 and FY17 carry large exceptional gains (FY16 EPS before exceptional items is 20.48 printed, 3.41 after dividing by 6), so FY17 growth and the FY16 base are not organic.
- Screener's EPS differs from the company's in every year shown (the cause is not established here; FY26 differs most); both are shown and the company's is the one the growth and CAGR tables use. Screener's 10-year EPS CAGR is not computed here.
- FY24 EPS growth is a base effect of the FY23 exceptional charge. FY26 EPS falls with a larger share count, so EPS growth and profit growth diverge for a non-operating reason.

## L7-44: Operating working-capital build and capex (with intangibles) against operating cash flow

**Formula in words.** OWC = inventories + trade receivables - trade payables, year end. Build = OWC this year - OWC last year (FY22-FY26), computed like for like: last year's payables are the ones the SAME report prints (its prior-year column), and the build on the earlier report's own year-end payables is shown beside it. Capex = purchase of property, plant and equipment, and again with the purchase of intangible assets added; the free-cash reading is operating cash flow less each measure. Operating cash flow is already AFTER the cash-flow statement's own working-capital lines, so the build is NOT subtracted from it again; the build is context, and the cash-flow lines are shown beside it. Gap % = |build - cash absorbed per the cash-flow statement| / the larger of the two; a year is flagged if the gap is over 20% or the sign differs.

**Caveats**

- A positive build is cash tied up in stock and customers net of suppliers; a negative build is cash released. A build is a balance-sheet change. Where it differs from the cash-flow lines by more than 20% or in sign (table 4), read it as: balance-sheet change includes acquisitions/consolidation changes; do not read as operating. FY23 is the clearest case: the Biologics deal consolidated the acquired business's inventories, receivables and payables, so the balance sheet shows inventories up while the cash-flow statement shows a decrease (table 5).
- Two bases for last year's trade payables exist for FY23 (39,831 in the FY23 report, 38,420 in the FY24 report; the other-creditors line reads 38,340 against 36,929, a 1,411 difference whose cause is not stated in the held pages). Only FY24's build is affected; table 1 prints both. Inventories and receivables prior-year columns were re-checked against the earlier report's own year end for FY23-FY26 and are identical.
- The cash-flow lines 'other assets' and 'trade payables, other liabilities and provisions' are wider than trade payables, so table 4 compares like with a slightly different thing; the sign and size of the gap are the reading, not the exact rupee.
- Operating profit before changes in operating assets and liabilities is printed on every cash-flow statement FY21-FY26 (table 3), so it is not derived here; the last column of table 3 is zero in every year, which ties the lines to the printed operating cash flow.
- Maintenance capex is not printed in any held file for FY21-FY26 (table 8). The only figure found is an FY18 estimate in the FY17 report; the later passages give total capex or a description, not a maintenance amount. Capex needed just to keep last year's volume is therefore not answered here.
- Table 7: the company-derived days come from the held derived-metrics file (receivable days on revenue, inventory and payable days on cost of goods); its payable days are blank for FY23 and FY25 (payables were not on file when it was built); this table holds payables for both years but does not add a cost-of-goods basis, so those two cells stay blank. Screener's days use its own definitions (its working capital days are not trade working capital), so a gap between the two sets is a definition gap, not an error in either.
- The held free_cash_flow for FY21-FY23 is PP&E only; the tie-out column shows where it matches column 'CFO - PP&E capex' or 'CFO - capex incl. intangibles'.

## L7-47: Value created per rupee kept, FY22 to FY26

**Formula in words.** Retained profit = sum of profit attributable to owners FY23-FY26 - sum of dividends paid FY23-FY26. Value created = market cap FY26 - market cap FY22. Ratio shown twice: unadjusted, and with the share-issue proceeds FY23-FY26 taken out of the numerator.

## L7-96: Price move before and after known events, against the Nifty 500

**Formula in words.** One stated rule picks the events (the first line under table 0 gives it in full, with the exclusion list): every record in the held feed whose exchange category or title words fall in one of the types in table 0, or, for a line whose title does not type it, whose held filing text does, plus results filing dates, meeting dates and the Mylan block. Day 0 = the first BIOCON session on or after the anchor date (the earliest public date held; table 1 says which date each row uses). Each window is close to close: run-up = close of day -1 over close of day -6 (the returns of days -5 to -1); day 0 = close of day 0 over close of day -1; day +1 = close of day +1 over close of day 0; days 0 to +5 = close of day +5 over close of day -1. The benchmark is the Nifty 500 (not the Nifty 50) over the same two dates. Excess = BIOCON move - Nifty 500 move, in percentage points. A window without both closes prints 'not held'. Rows of the BIOCON Yahoo file with Volume 0 (days with no NSE session) are dropped from both price series before any window is read. The match is by DATE only.

**Inputs (held)**

| Input | Period | Value | Unit | Basis | Source file |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BIOCON daily close (1717 sessions, 2019-12-02 to 2026-11-09; 2 zero-volume non-session date(s) dropped: 2025-03-18, 2026-01-15) | all | values listed in the result table | Rs | exchange price | soic-ladder/docs/idea/case-study/BIOCON/data/macro/BIOCON-daily.csv |
| Nifty 500 daily close (2689 sessions, 2016-01-01 to 2026-11-09; 1 zero-volume non-session date(s) dropped: 2025-03-18) | all | values listed in the result table | index points | n/a | soic-ladder/docs/idea/case-study/BIOCON/data/macro/niftyindices-price-NIFTY500-2016..2024.json + niftyindices-NIFTY500-price-2025-2026.json |

**Caveats**

- SELECTION RULE, final: a feed record is a row when (1) its exchange category is one of Acquisition, Agreements, Pendency of Litigation(s)/dispute(s) or the outcome impacting the Company, Action(s) taken or orders passed, Rumour Verification - Regulation 30(11), News Verification, Credit Rating, or it is an Issue / Allotment of Securities whose text says preferential or qualified institution; or (2) its category is one of General Updates; General updates; Outcome of Board Meeting; Press Release; Press Release (Revised); Updates and the words of its TITLE fit one type of the closed list in table 0 (a board outcome counts only as an acquisition or divestment or a securities issue); or (3) its category is one of those, its title fits no type, and the title (else the first sentence) of its HELD FILING text fits exactly one type (the 'settle' tie-break settles a tie; otherwise two types is 'ambiguous', none is 'no keyword', and no held text is 'file missing', and each of those with a type word, a near word or a generic title is a line of table 3; a held filing read with no type word and a descriptive feed title is counted, not listed); records of one date merge into one row; results dates, meeting dates and the Mylan block are rows of their own. EXCLUDED, the exact list: by name, Financial Result Updates (a results document, not an event (the results filing dates are rows of their own)); Investor Presentation (a deck that re-presents news already public, not an event of its own); Disclosure under SEBI Takeover Regulations (a shareholder's own disclosure, not an action of the company); and every other category, not read: Amendment to AOA/MOA (1), Analysts/Institutional Investor Meet/Con. Call Updates (237), Annual Disclosure (1), Annual General Meeting (4), Annual Secretarial Compliance Report (1), Appointment (13), Board Meeting Intimation (1), Certificate under SEBI (Depositories and Participants) Regulations, 2018 (18), Cessation (4), Change in Auditors (2), Change in Company Secretary/Compliance Officer (1), Change in Director(s) (10), Change in Management (1), Closure of trading window (2), Communication to shareholders as per Reg 30 (2), Copy of Newspaper Publication (45), Date of payment of dividend (1), Declaration for audit reports with unmodified opinion(s) (1), Dividend (4), Dividend Updates (1), Integrated Filing- Financial (1), Issue of Duplicate Share Certificate (1), Loss of Share Certificates (7), Monitoring Agency Report (5), Newspaper Advertisements (6), Options to purchase securities (1), Record Date (4), Redemption (2), Related Party Transaction (2), Related Party Transactions (4), Resignation (7), Resignation of Director/KMP/SMP (1), Resignation of Independent director (1), Schedule of Analysts/Institutional Investor Meet/Con. Call (47), Shareholders meeting (36), Trading Window (16), Transcript of Analysts/Institutional Investor Meet/Con. Call (3).
- The feed holds 1033 records, counted by the key (date, attachment link), a record with no link keyed by (date, category, text) instead; plain (date, link) would give 1030, because 3 link-less lines on a date they share with another link-less line would then count as one; 188 fall in a claimed type and 188 of them are in a row below; 0 typed records have no row. 50 further records hold a keyword of a claimed type (in their feed text, or in the title or first sentence of their filing) but are untyped; each is in table 3 with its reason (6 a board outcome or securities filing, 31 a category the rule does not read, 7 an ambiguous filing, 6 an excluded category). The gap is computed, not asserted: for each type, typed records with no row plus untyped records holding its keyword (a record holding two types' keywords counts under both). Real gap by type: regulator letter or inspection 1; credit-rating action 0; securities issue 2; litigation or settlement 0; acquisition or divestment 12; agreement 1; approval 41; exchange action 0; news verification 0. A gap count is not a count of missed events: table 3 gives each record's reason (a shareholder's own disclosure, a category the rule does not read, a corporate rather than regulator approval, an ambiguous filing ...).
- 34 'Company Statement' lines carry no title in the feed (only the words 'Company Statement', at most a date). 2 of them (2026-09-25 and 2026-10-14) are typed from filing titles read by hand; of the other 32, 27 are typed from their held filing text by the keyword rule below (2 agreement, 13 approval, 10 regulator letter or inspection, 2 litigation or settlement) and 5 stay untyped; each of those is in table 3 with its reason.
- The same filing rule read every other line the title rule did not type, in the categories it reads (349 lines): 7 ambiguous, 6 blocked, 48 file missing, 238 no keyword, 50 typed. 50 lines are newly typed from their held filing text (5 acquisition or divestment, 24 approval, 1 credit-rating action, 20 regulator letter or inspection); 212 lines had their held filing read, hold no type word or near word and name a subject in their feed title, so they are counted here and not listed; 37 lines type from their filing text but sit in a category that cannot carry the type (a board outcome, a securities filing, or a category the rule does not read) and are in table 3.
- The filing-text rule: the type comes from the filing's TITLE (the 'w.r.t.' title in the cover letter, or the heading); if the title holds no type word, from the FIRST SENTENCE of the statement. One type of the closed list must fit; none is 'no keyword'; two or more is settled by the tie-break below, or is 'ambiguous' and stays untyped. the very same types and keyword list as the title rule (one list in the script); the phrases 'Biologics License Application', 'pre-approval' (also split as 'pre -approval'), 'pre-license' / 'Pre-Licensing' and 'inspection authority' are blanked first because they name a filing, a kind of inspection or an agency, not an event.
- Tie-break (one rule, applied to every filing the same way): when two or more types fit and the first sentence says 'settle' or 'settlement', the type is litigation or settlement; any other tie stays 'ambiguous'. Titled records have no ties to break, because their one ordered first-hit rule already puts litigation or settlement ahead of acquisition, agreement and approval. The tie-break decided 1 record(s): 2024-03-04 (Biocon Biologics Ltd (BBL), a globally integrated biosimilars company ).
- Own dates: 0 of the 77 typed filings print a date (letter or dateline) earlier than the feed row, so their windows are anchored on it; the rest print the feed date and are anchored on the feed row.
- Merging: of the 50 newly typed lines, 42 dates opened a new row, 5 lines merged into a row that already existed that date (4 dates; the row keeps its first record and lists the merged line in its description and its file in its source), and 3 merged into a row opened the same day by another newly typed line. 0 newly typed line(s) fall on a results, meeting or block date (they keep a row of their own, so that date's windows repeat).
- 126 other records hold a type word or a near word (approval, rating, agreement, partnership, integration, acquisition ...), sit in an excluded or unread category, are ambiguous, or have no held text, and are not taken: listed in table 3 so a gap can be read.
- Stated exclusion, one list: Financial Result Updates: 0 record(s) hold a type keyword (none), all listed in table 3; Investor Presentation: 1 record(s) hold a type keyword (2022-03-01), all listed in table 3; Disclosure under SEBI Takeover Regulations: 5 record(s) hold a type keyword (2026-07-28, 2026-06-15, 2025-04-08, 2024-04-03, 2023-04-25), all listed in table 3.
- Guard: 0 record(s) whose held filing text types as a claimed type have neither a row nor a table 3 line.
- Event types: results filings, AGM/EGM meeting dates, the Mylan block, and every feed record the selection rule types, by its title or, when the title does not type it, by its held filing text (approvals, FDA letters and inspections, securities issues by QIP or preferential allotment, credit-rating actions, acquisitions and divestments, agreements, litigation and settlements, exchange actions, news verifications). Only dates that a held file prints are used; none is estimated.
- Two dates, one anchor: the date on a feed row is when the exchange record was filed; an event can be public earlier (a press release dated the day before, a letter to the exchanges on a Sunday). The windows are anchored on the EARLIEST PUBLIC date held: the event's own date when a held record shows it as a public record dated before the feed row, otherwise the feed date. A rating agency's action date is shown but is not used as the anchor, because the agency's letter goes to the issuer and no public record is held for that day.
- NSE filing times are often after the close, so a move can sit on day +1 rather than day 0; the 'Day 0 note' column flags the cases the feed time shows (the feed time is not used when the anchor is the event's own date).
- A move on a date is a co-occurrence, not a cause: other news, index events and the whole sector move on the same days. The held results-date table in price-technicals.md is against the Nifty 50; this one is against the Nifty 500.
- Several feed items fall on one date (a board outcome, an allotment and an acquisition on the same evening); they are merged into one row so a single window is not counted three times, and every type that day carries is counted in the selection table. 7 row(s) share their anchor date with an earlier row (most are an event on a results day), so their windows repeat.

## L2-26: Which cost or revenue line moved the latest margin (bridge, Q1 FY27 and Q2 FY27 against the year-ago quarters)

**Caveats**

- The operating margin here is revenue from operations less materials (consumed, purchased, change in stock), employee benefits and other expenses, plus the recovery from co-development partners; other income, finance costs and depreciation sit below it. It is this table's own definition, not the company's EBITDA, which includes other income (derived metric ebitda_company_basis).
- Each quarter uses its own filing's columns. The Sep-2026 filing restates the Jun-2026 quarter (employee benefits 9,281 against 9,161 filed in June; other expenses 10,947 against 11,067), a 120 shift between the two lines with no change in the total; the Jun-2025 quarter is shown as the June filing printed it because the September filing holds no restated Jun-2025 column. The FY26 column in the Sep filing is restated the same way (employee 35,521 against 35,080).
- A change in a line's share of revenue is a fact about the filing; it is not a cause. A date match between a line and a call sentence is not a cause either.

## L3-28: Cause of the latest margin change and the line showing it

**Formula in words.** Take the cost line whose share of revenue moved most year on year (from the L2-26 bridge); show employee plus other expenses beside it; quote management's own sentences and the FY26 annual-report paragraph unchanged.

**Inputs (held)**

| Input | Period | Value | Unit | Basis | Source file |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| consolidated statement, quarter ended 30 June 2026 (columns: Jun 2026, Mar 2026, Jun 2025) | file | read | Rs million | consolidated | soic-ladder/docs/idea/case-study/BIOCON/data/excerpts/consolidated-2026-06-30.txt |
| consolidated statement, quarter ended 30 September 2026 (columns: Sep 2026, Jun 2026 as restated, Sep 2025) | file | read | Rs million | consolidated | soic-ladder/docs/idea/case-study/BIOCON/data/excerpts/consolidated-2026-09-30.txt |

**Computed result, DERIVED**

*Largest-moving cost line, year on year (DERIVED)*

| Quarter | Line | Change in % of revenue, points | Effect on operating margin, points | Employee + other expenses, change in Rs million % | Employee + other expenses, change in % of revenue, points | Operating margin before other income, change in points |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 FY27 (quarter ended 30 June 2026) | materials, net (the three above) | -1.80 | 1.80 | 12.05 | 0.85 | 0.10 |
| Q2 FY27 (quarter ended 30 September 2026) | other expenses | 3.16 | -3.16 | 8.04 | 2.57 | -0.52 |

*Management's words (call 2026-08-06, Q1 FY27) and the FY26 annual report; sentences copied unchanged, checked to be in the file*

| Sentence | Check |
|---|---|
| And I think you should note the work that we have done on opex across all three companies from quarter four to quarter one and even year-on-year, you will see a significant drop in the operating expenses. | verbatim in soic-ladder/docs/idea/case-study/BIOCON/data/management-text/call-2026-08-06.md |
| We focused on getting cost out of the system that's helped as well and operating leverage has started to play. | verbatim in soic-ladder/docs/idea/case-study/BIOCON/data/management-text/call-2026-08-06.md |
| The improvement was driven by a favorable revenue mix and operating leverage benefits. | verbatim in soic-ladder/docs/idea/case-study/BIOCON/annual-report/FY26/p067.md |

**Caveats**

- The Q2 FY27 call (after the 2026-11-04 filing) is not held: no management words about the Sep-2026 quarter are on file, so that quarter's cause is the filed lines alone.
- The FY26 annual-report paragraph (p067) says margins expanded by about 200 basis points to 22% on a like-to-like basis ('lower 13% on a reported basis'); the reported margin on total income fell from 26.56% (FY25) to 21.99% (FY26) in the held derived metric. It is a full-year paragraph, not the latest quarter's.
- The call sentences are management's explanation, not a verified cause. The CFO's 'opex ... even year-on-year' sentence does not define 'operating expenses'; the two lines shown here (employee, other expenses) are not a test of it.

## L2-57: ROE before exceptional items and on tangible equity; buyback

**Formula in words.** ROE (owners) = profit to owners / average of opening and closing equity attributable to owners, x 100, on the deck's reported net profit and on its net profit before exceptional items (Rs crore x 10 = Rs million). ROE (tangible) = profit for the year (all holders) / tangible total equity (total equity less goodwill and intangibles), closing and average, n/m where the base is zero or negative.

- A return over a base that is a few per cent of total equity is not a return a reader can use: FY25's closing-base figure (218%) divides by a tangible equity of 6,549, which the 'tangible equity as % of total equity' column shows is about 2% of total equity.
- Goodwill is about 72% of total equity in FY23 against 0.3% in FY22 (held metric), so tangible equity is negative in FY23 and FY24 and the tangible ROE is n/m there; the held files do not say what caused the jump.
- No buyback payment line is in the held cash-flow table (only share-issue proceeds); the secretarial auditor lists the SEBI buyback regulations as 'Not Applicable to the Company during the Audit Period' in each year FY22 to FY26. That is the auditor's list, not a cash-flow check; a line missing from a table of selected lines is not proof of none.
- The deck's exceptional items (net of tax and NCI) were charges in FY22, FY23, FY24 and FY26 and a gain in FY25, so the before-exceptional ROE sits above the reported one in FY22, FY23, FY24 and FY26 and below it in FY25. Profit for the year is before NCI and tangible equity includes NCI's share, so the two tangible bases match each other and not the owners' basis above.

## L3-22: Adjusted PAT and adjusted growth

**Formula in words.** Adjusted PAT (after tax and non-controlling interest) = the deck's printed 'Net Profit (Before exceptional items)', Rs crore. Growth = this year / last year - 1. The bar is the rulebook's PAT growth rule, shown as a comparison only. Tie-out: before-exceptional + exceptional (net of tax and NCI) - reported should be 0, and reported should match the annual report's profit to owners / 10.

## L7-14: Which EPS for the P/E: reported against before exceptional items

**Formula in words.** EPS before exceptional items = reported basic EPS x (deck net profit before exceptional items / deck net profit reported). P/E = the last weekly close on or before 31 March / EPS, on the reported and on the adjusted EPS.

## L4-42: Preferential and QIP allottees and the lock-in end date

**Formula in words.** For each equity allotment in the feed: the 90th NSE session after the allotment date, counted on the held bhavcopy calendar. The 90-trading-day term is the one the notices state for the allottees' PRE-preferential holding, counted from the date of trading approval. The date of trading approval is not held, so this is the EARLIEST possible end, not the end.

| soic-ladder/docs/idea/case-study/BIOCON/data/filings/text/2025-12-07_BIOCON_07122025141941_Biocon_EGM_Intimation/p024.txt | The entire pre-preferential allotment shareholding, if any, of the Proposed Allottees shall be locked-in from the Relevant Date up to a period of 90 (ninety) trading days from the date of trading approval, as per the SEBI ICDR Regulations. | verbatim in soic-ladder/docs/idea/case-study/BIOCON/data/filings/text/2025-12-07_BIOCON_07122025141941_Biocon_EGM_Intimation/p024.txt |

**Caveats**

- NOT COMPUTABLE as an end date: (1) the lock-in for the NEW shares is stated only as 'such period as specified under Chapter V of the SEBI ICDR Regulations', with no number in any held file, and this file does not supply one from memory; (2) the date of trading approval, the start of the stated 90 sessions, is not held; (3) QIP shares carry no lock-in term in the held notices.
- Held notices name a lock-in only in the EGM notice of 2025-12-07 (the preferential issue allotted 2026-01-05) and in the postal-ballot notice of 2026-05-09 (p039, the same two sentences; that it is the notice behind the 2026-06-29 allotment is not confirmed in the held files); no QIP document with a lock-in term is held.
- The 2025-06-19 QIP allotment (97 qualified buyers) is known from the EGM notice text, not from a feed row, and the held calendar starts after it, so no session count is possible for it.

## L4-46: ROE before and two years after each issue

**Formula in words.** Each issue's fiscal year t is read from its date (April to March). ROE at t-1, t and t+2 are held metrics (average-equity ROE / closing-equity ROE); where t+2 is after FY26 the issue has not matured and the cell says so.

**Caveats**

- ROE FY21 is held on closing equity only (no FY20 balance), so an average-equity ROE at FY21 prints 'not held'.
- The two 2023 rows are debt allotments (non-convertible securities), shown because the feed lists them under 'Allotment of Securities'; an equity issue changes the denominator of ROE, a debt issue does not, so only the equity rows speak to the question.
- The FY26 issues are the three equity events of 2025-26 (QIP June 2025, preferential January 2026 to Mylan, QIP January 2026); the June 2026 preferential falls in FY27, whose ROE is not held. ROE after an issue is a date-ordered fact; it is not shown to be caused by the issue.
- Share-issue proceeds in FY23 (12,368) are a subsidiary's issue to outside holders, per the L7-47 note, not the company's own.

## L5-63: Retained earnings against the change in market value over rolling five years

**Formula in words.** Ratio = change in market cap / change in consolidated RETAINED earnings (the closing balance printed in the statement of changes in equity, not other equity). Only one five-year window can be formed from the held years (FY21 to FY26); the one-year steps are shown too. Other equity and owners' equity sit beside it because an earlier version of this row used other equity as a stand-in. Close convention: retained earnings = the closing balance at 31 March of each year (the statement of changes in equity's 'Balance at March 31'); market cap = the last WEEKLY close on or before 31 March (a week-ending date, 26 March 2021 for FY21) x shares at 31 March, as in the held derived-metrics 'market_cap'; the last DAILY close on or before 31 March is shown beside it.

**Caveats**

- Every balance is a figure printed on a held page (OCR text of the annual report; the statement is a rotated table, so cells are spread over lines). Years with two or three pages are read on each and kept only if the pages agree. The FY21 report itself is not held; Mar-21 is the opening column of the FY22 and FY23 reports.
- Retained earnings also moved for reasons other than profit: the step column 'Change in retained not from that profit' is largest in FY23 (+87,959) and FY26 (-53,527). On FY26's page a (53,633) cell sits in the retained-earnings column; by position among the 13 listed movement rows (the total-other-equity column carries 13 movement values against 13 row labels) it falls on 'Loss on purchase of shares in a subsidiary'; that positional read is not independently confirmed. The change in retained earnings is therefore not 'profit kept after dividends'; L7-47 holds the profit-less-dividends version for FY22 to FY26.
- Other equity is not retained earnings: it also holds securities premium, reserves for share-based payment, hedging, currency translation and others (each is a column of the same printed statement); the 'retained as % of other equity' column shows how much of it is retained.
- Market cap FY26 rests on 1,621,073,650 shares (after the issues of 2025-26) against 1,200,567,573 before, so the unadjusted change includes new capital; the net-of-proceeds line takes the share-issue cash out and nothing else.
- Windows starting before FY21 are not possible: the share count behind market cap is not held before FY21 (price-only history is in the ten-year file), and no retained-earnings page is held before Mar-21.

## L5-64: Returns by segment and new capital against old

**Formula in words.** Net segment assets (capital employed) = segment assets - segment liabilities at year end (note 40, the CODM's table). Return = segment result / net segment assets, and (segment result + segment finance costs) / net segment assets. New against old = the FY25 to FY26 change in net assets set beside the change in segment result.

**Inputs (held)**

| Input | Period | Value | Unit | Basis | Source file |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| segment tables, note 40 (FY26, FY25 on p413-p414; FY24, FY23 on p368) | years | 4 | tables | consolidated | soic-ladder/docs/idea/case-study/BIOCON/annual-report/FY26/p413.md, p414.md; soic-ladder/docs/idea/case-study/BIOCON/annual-report/FY24/p368.md |
| bs.total_assets | FY21 | 185,223 | Rs million | consolidated | roast-room/docs/company-record/biocon/ar-tables-FY21-FY26.csv (bs.total_assets, filed; AR pdf p264, p308, p346, p348, p362) |
| bs.total_assets | FY22 | 203,940 | Rs million | consolidated | roast-room/docs/company-record/biocon/ar-tables-FY21-FY26.csv (bs.total_assets, filed; AR pdf p264, p308, p346, p348, p362) |
| bs.total_assets | FY23 | 520,428 | Rs million | consolidated | roast-room/docs/company-record/biocon/ar-tables-FY21-FY26.csv (bs.total_assets, filed; AR pdf p264, p308, p346, p348, p362) |
| bs.total_assets | FY24 | 560,707 | Rs million | consolidated | roast-room/docs/company-record/biocon/ar-tables-FY21-FY26.csv (bs.total_assets, filed; AR pdf p264, p308, p346, p348, p362) |
| bs.total_assets | FY25 | 587,973 | Rs million | consolidated | roast-room/docs/company-record/biocon/ar-tables-FY21-FY26.csv (bs.total_assets, filed; AR pdf p264, p308, p346, p348, p362) |
| bs.total_assets | FY26 | 636,506 | Rs million | consolidated | roast-room/docs/company-record/biocon/ar-tables-FY21-FY26.csv (bs.total_assets, filed; AR pdf p264, p308, p346, p348, p362) |

**Computed result, DERIVED**

*Return on net segment assets by year (DERIVED, Rs million)*

| Year | Segment | Segment assets | Segment liabilities | Net segment assets | Segment result | Result / net assets % | (Result + finance costs) / net assets % |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FY23 | Generics | 58,526 | 17,496 | 41,030 | 3,921 | 9.56 | 9.39 |

| FY24 | CRDMO | 61,516 | 18,939 | 42,577 | 6,319 | 14.84 | 13.73 |

| FY25 | CRDMO | 67,959 | 20,691 | 47,268 | 6,279 | 13.28 | 12.16 |
| FY26 | Generics | 89,926 | 37,561 | 52,365 | -1,348 | -2.57 | -3.73 |

*New capital against old, FY25 to FY26 (DERIVED, Rs million)*

| Segment | Net assets FY25 | Net assets FY26 | Change in net assets (new capital) | New capital as % of old | Change in segment result | Change in result per rupee of new capital % | Return on old capital FY25 % |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

**Caveats**

- Segment liabilities are the group's liabilities allocated to segments, borrowings included: FY26 total assets 636,506 less total liabilities 270,291 is 366,215, the held balance-sheet total equity. Net segment assets are therefore an equity-like base, and the result (after finance costs) over it is a pre-tax return on that base; the second column adds finance costs back.
- FY23 and FY24 name the third segment 'Research' (shown here as CRDMO) and carry a 'Novels' segment with no assets but a segment result (4,736 in FY24, 318 in FY23, mostly other income), which has no capital to earn on and is left out of the table. The segment result includes other income and, in FY25, large Biosimilars other income (11,275); the table does not strip it out.
- The year-end base is used, not an average, because FY22 and earlier segment tables are not read here. A change in result over a change in capital is dominated by the result where capital barely moved (read it with the 'new capital as % of old' column) and is n/m where capital fell.

## L5-85: Net insider buying or selling by quarter, beside management's statements

**Formula in words.** Group the PIT disclosure rows by calendar quarter of the trade date. Open market = Market Purchase and Market Sale only; ESOP = shares acquired on exercise (not a market buy); inter-se = transfers among promoters (net of buy and sell); pledge rows counted, not netted; promoters + promoter group + KMP = their net shares in every mode. Net = bought less sold, shares; value in Rs crore. Beside each held quarter: every sentence of the held earnings-call text (management's words only) that holds one of the search words, quoted with file and line.

- Words searched in the call text (case-insensitive): promoter|insider|pledg|skin in the game|my own (?:shares|stake|money)|(?:buy|buying|bought|sell|selling|sold) (?:more |our |my )?(?:own )?shares. 1 sentence(s) match across 14 calls, 0 of them in a quarter with insider rows. Calls held after 2024-Q1 (call-2024-05-16.md, call-2024-08-09.md, call-2025-01-31.md, call-2025-05-09.md, call-2025-08-08.md, call-2025-11-12.md, call-2026-02-13.md, call-2026-05-08.md, call-2026-08-06.md) cannot be set beside any insider row because none is held for their quarters. Quarters before the first call held (call-2022-11-15.md) have insider rows and no call.
- A sentence that holds a search word is a candidate, not a statement about insider dealing: read the quote. The annual-report prose (statements-ar-fy22 to fy26, section T11) is not call text and was not searched here.
- 'Not stated' (117 of 206 rows) is NSE's blank in the person-category field, so those rows cannot be assigned to promoters, KMP or employees; they are in the open-market and ESOP columns but not in the promoter + KMP column.
- The open-market column is mostly sales (84 'Market Sale' rows against 3 'Market Purchase' rows, the three purchases totalling 2,000,000 shares in category 'Other'), and ESOP exercises are not purchases, so net buying by quarter is not 'insiders buying'. No buy or sell view is drawn.

## L6-68: Breadth reading and phase

**Formula in words.** Newest session in the derived breadth file: stocks above their own 200-session average / stocks measured. Phase = where that share sits against the course's stated bands (H4-177), read from the framework file, not from the rulebook.

- The breadth figure is DERIVED by this repo's fetch job from unadjusted closes (a split or bonus in the window reads as a fall), today's Nifty 500 list is applied to every date (survivors only), and only 313 sessions are held, so the 200-session average exists only for the last 113. It is not an NSE statistic. No view on the market is drawn.

## L7-10: Minority (non-controlling) interest, valued

**Formula in words.** Minority at market = 47.6% (note 39: ownership held by non-controlling interest, March 2026) x Syngene's market cap. Set beside book NCI (balance sheet and note 39(b)). Share count: market cap / price, from screener's rounded figures. Biocon's own 52.4% at the same market value is shown for scale.

- Biocon Biologics' 1.3% minority is not valued: BBL is unlisted and no price for it is held. A market value for a listed subsidiary's minority is a value on one day and is not what NCI would be sold for.
- Book NCI total fell from 60,685 (FY25) to 25,897 (FY26); note 39 prints the Biologics minority falling from 23.2% to 1.3% over the same year, and Syngene's accumulated NCI is 23,044 of the 25,897, so the March 2026 book NCI mostly sits in Syngene.

## L7-46: Retained earnings and book value per share by year

**Formula in words.** BVPS (Rs) = equity attributable to owners (Rs million) x 1,000,000 / shares outstanding at 31 March. Retained earnings per share and other equity per share = each x 1,000,000 / the same shares. P/B = the 31-March weekly close / BVPS, beside the held price-to-book. Cumulative retained = retained earnings at the year-end less the Mar-21 balance, set beside the rise in market cap over the same span. Close convention: retained earnings = the closing balance at 31 March of each year (the statement of changes in equity's 'Balance at March 31'); market cap = the last WEEKLY close on or before 31 March (a week-ending date, 26 March 2021 for FY21) x shares at 31 March, as in the held derived-metrics 'market_cap'; the last DAILY close on or before 31 March is shown beside it.

**Caveats**

- The step asks for a ten-year span; six year-ends are held (FY21 to FY26). FY16 to FY20 retained earnings, reserves and share counts are not held (shares before FY21 are not on file), so the ten-year span is 'not held' and the FY21 to FY26 row is the only five-year window. The step's bar is Re 1 of market value per Re 1 retained (example B1-179, B9-91; no rulebook rule): the FY26 row is the comparison.
- The cumulative change in retained earnings is a change in a balance, not profit kept after dividends: see L5-63's one-year step table, column 'Change in retained not from that profit', for the years where it is large. The FY21 share count is the Sep-2021 filing reused (nothing earlier on file) and FY26 uses the 31-March-2026 filing's count (1,621,073,650); screener's figure is on its own share count and date. Book value here is after the NCI split (owners' share only).
